### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月 ， 降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險 ， 支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續 ，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方 ，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高 ， 令人民幣由升轉貶至6.7200 上方 ； 臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場來看 ， 美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢 ， 不過10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假 ， 預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁 ， 帶動USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪 ， 主要原因如下 ： 一 、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.50-159.00 
前週交易區間 
156.35 158.45

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1400 
前週交易區間 
1.1214 1.1391

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.29-115.10.02 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週為提存期底 ， 繼央行上週大砍存單效應持續發酵 ， 市
場資金供給仍充裕，惟需求疲弱，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率高位較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投仍冷清，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三於處所成交在 1.82%，10 年
期指標券則未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價為 1.95%。 
第四季台債發行量雖偏少 ， 惟美伊和談依舊陷入僵局 ， 國際油價續處高位 ， 通
膨疑慮尚存，國際債市走勢仍偏空，預期台債殖利率短線將維持高檔盤整格
局，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.95%~2%。

研 究  報 告                
南亞科(2408.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
價格與⻑約重議價推升獲利，AI 需求⽀撐中⻑期成⻑  
 投資評價與建議 
受惠與記憶體供不應求持續，目前⻑約覆蓋率約50%～70%之間，合約期限從
季度到⻑達3年不等，其中，3年期合約佔總產出20%以上，有助營運能見度提
升；另隨著AI應用占比提升，有助降低記憶體景氣循環影響。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26反應⻑期合約重新簽訂之調整，ASP季增超過60%： 3Q26營收季增
61.9%至1,336.5億元，年增611.7%，受惠平均售價季增60+%，其⻑約貢獻超
過⼀半以上；位元出貨量季減中個位數百分比，⽑利率79.5%攀升至85.7%，
營業淨利率80%，季增6.3ppts，營業費用75.88億元，費用增加主因當期及未
來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利876.27億元，稅後EPS 25.4元，優於預期。 
4Q26供不應求延續，預期ASP持續上揚、出貨持平：展望4Q26，公司預期市
場供不應求持續，ASP持續向上，惟在位元出貨量，由於目前庫存處於低檔，
預期未來數個季度的位元出貨量將保持平穩，待4Q27新廠開始投產，預估
4Q26營收季增21.7%至1,626.7億元，⽑利率86.6%，稅後31.29元。 
随新廠產能開出，目標AI基礎設施逐步提升：新廠規劃1Q27裝機，因無大
幅增加產出的空間，加上庫存處於低水位，預期2027年初出貨量持平。南
亞科目前AI基礎設施相關營收占比已超過20%，含蓋通訊、儲存、系統管
理、eSSD以及RDIMM模組等，2030年以前目標提升至40～50%。 
 投資風險 
AI基礎建設投資放緩、客製化AI/WoW產品進度不如預期

| 營運概況  
3Q26 反應⻑期合約重新簽訂之調整 ， ASP 季增超過 60% ：3Q26 營收季增 61.9%至 1,336.5 億元 ， 年增 611.7% ，
受惠平均售價季增 60+%，其⻑約貢獻超過⼀半以上；位元出貨量季減中個位數百分比，⽑利率 79%攀升至 85.7%，
營業淨利 1,068.9 億元，營業淨利率 80%，季增 6.3ppts，營業費用 75.88 億元，費用增加主因當期及未來的員
工薪酬增加 ， 稅後淨利 876.27 億元 ， 淨利率 65.6% ， 稅後 EPS 25.4 元 ， 優於預期 。 產品結構部份 ， DDR4/LPDDR4
佔比逾 60%，DDR5 約佔 10%；此外，AI 基礎設施(含伺服器、網路、儲存及系統管理)占營收超過 20%。 
4Q26 供不應求延續，預期 ASP 持續上揚、出貨持平：展望 4Q26，公司預期市場供不應求持續，ASP 持續向上，
惟在位元出貨量，由於目前庫存處於低檔，預期未來數個季度的位元出貨量將保持平穩，直到 4Q27 新廠開始投
產，預估 4Q26 營收季增 21.7%至 1,626.7 億元，⽑利率 86.6%，稅後 31.29 元。此外，就供需市況部份，隨著 AI
運算主導 DRAM 需求成⻑ ， 延伸至網路 、 儲存及系統管理等應用 ， 推升高容量 、 高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求 ，
而傳統應用因受限於供給不足加上報價調漲，轉而降低規格、延⻑⽣命週期來因應；另供給則因新廠建置、設備
交期、製程導入，均需要較⻑的前置時間，預期供不應求狀況在 2027 年至 2028 年前難以解決。 
随新廠產能開出，目標 AI 基礎設施應用占比逐步提升：產能規劃方面，新廠規劃 1Q27 裝機，因無大幅增加產出
的空間，再加上庫存消耗後，正處低水位，預期 2027 年初出貨量持平，隨著 4Q27 新廠開始投產後有望逐步提
升，目標 2028 年達每月投片量３萬片，2030 年年底達到每月投片量５萬片。此外，第三代 10 奈米級製程(1C)
預計 1Q27 開始客戶驗證，並同步開發第四代(1D)、第五代(1E)製程及 EUV，均按計畫推進。另規劃於屏東科學
園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠，預計 2028 年年底前導入矽穿孔(TSV)、晶圓堆疊等 3D IC 先進技術，未來擴建
皆以客戶⻑期合約的 AI 專案為目標。南亞科目前 AI 基礎設施相關營收占比已超過 20%，含蓋通訊、儲存、系統
管理、eSSD 以及 RDIMM 模組等，2030 年以前目標提升至 40～50%；此外，目前⻑約覆蓋率約 50%～70%之
間，合約期限從季度到⻑達 3 年不等，其中，3 年期合約佔總產出 20%以上，有助營運能見度提升；另與客戶合
作 AI 相關晶圓對晶圓技術 ASIC 專案，應用範圍涵蓋雲端 AI 運算、邊緣運算及物理 AI，已有營收貢獻，雖然目
前規模不大，後續業務發展值得關注。預估 2027 年營收 7,378.76 億元，⽑利率 87.1%，稅後 EPS 141.9 元，維
持「買進」之投資建議，目標價 710 元(3.8 倍 PBR*2027BVPS(F))。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Given its longstanding position in the global foundry industry, 
ever challenging TSMC’s leadership during the late 1990s, we believe 
UMC stands well to benefit from a ripple effect in demand spurred by AI 
scaling, as detailed in the main section of this report. We expect UMC’s 
wafer loadings to reach 100% from 1Q27, with price hikes to go through 
(see Price hike to go through? Still likely, 31 July). That said, we believe the 
equity market has priced in this AI strength on extra volume and pricing 
power for UMC. Hence, we stick with our Hold (3) rating on the counter.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 preview and 4Q26 outlook. We expect UMC to 
report 3Q26 net profit of TWD16.4bn (+9% YoY), varying not much from the 
Bloomberg consensus. Its monthly revenue finished at c.TWD49bn QTD, 
reaching 65% of our/consensus 3Q26 estimates, which we consider 
tracking inline. For 4Q26, we expect UMC to earn net profit of TWD16bn, 
13% below consensus, as the street seems to be assuming higher non-
operating gains. UMC is scheduled to report results on 28 October; we will 
be offering updates in due course.

<=28nm ramp on AI scaling and fab optimisation. UMC’s 28/22nm-and-
below revenue contribution has hovered around the high-30% range over 
the past 2 years, after meaningful ramp during the COVID period on 
broadened demand applications from display drivers and TV SoCs to 
power management. With AI scaling creating a ripple effect in companion 
chip demand for connectivity (ie, silicon photonics) and power (ie, server 
PMICs), we believe UMC is well positioned to benefit from any spillover 
demand, with the strength augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation, which 
could strengthen UMC’s pricing power. Given that UMC’s wafer loadings 
are likely to reach 100% from 1Q27, we expect its price hikes to go through 
and model increases of 2-5%, effective from 1Q27, across nodes related to 
AI applications. This should resume its <=28nm ramp to exceed a half of 
revenue in 2028. All in, we raise our 2027-28E EPS by 22%.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we remain 
comfortable with our Hold (3) rating for UMC, as we believe its AI strength 
is already priced in. We raise our 12M TP to TWD145 (previous: TWD108), 
based on our higher ROE-adjusted PBR target of 4.2x (previous: 3.3x) 
applied to 1-year-forward BVPS on a higher ROE trajectory. Upside/ 
downside risks would be any price hikes above our expectations/ 
geopolitical overhangs (war, FX, interest rate) weighing on end-demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS do not vary much from consensus, 
indicating that UMC’s AI strength is already reflected in its valuation.

9 October 2026

United Microelectronics

A beneficiary of the AI ripple effect, but …

➢ UMC looks set to benefit from AI demand spillover 
➢ Yet we believe the AI ripple effect is being priced in 
➢ Maintain our Hold (3) call, with a higher 12M TP of TWD145

Company profile 
Incorporated in Taiwan, United Microelectronics Corp (UMC) is one of the leading semiconductor 
foundries in the world and second largest in Taiwan. UMC offers a series of foundry services from 
photo-mask, wafer fabrication to wafer probe & test. Its wafer fabs are spread over Taiwan, China and 
Singapore, with technology offerings ranging from 0.25u and above, 0.18/0.15u, 0.13u, 90nm, 65nm, 
45/40nm, 28/22nm to 14nm FF.

United Microelectronics (2303 TT): 9 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 Oct 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company 
ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
UMC’s Board of Directors consists of nine members whose expertise covers industry, academia,
finance, accounting, and management. Among these directors, six are independent directors,
representing 67% of total directors in 2024. The percentage of independent directors is high
relative to the regulatory requirement of at least 20% or at least 2 independent directors.
Although some of UMC's executive management are also directors, UMC's Chairman and
President are not the same person. Considering this and its relative-high percentage of
independent directors, we believe the board can perform its functionalities well
Capital management 2 It kept its dividend payout ratios above 50% in 2017-25, which is at a healthy, sustainable level.Overall, we see the balance between investment and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were around 1-2% of its total revenue each year. The percentage isinsignificant and thus we see limited risk for UMC.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
UMC has constructed an evaluation framework for reviewing the sustainable development of its
supply chains based on UMC’s GG-026-004-C sustainable supply-chain management review
guidelines to ensure its suppliers fulfil environmental protection, social responsibility, labour and
human rights, safety & health and sustainable development guidelines. We believe these efforts
could reduce its supply-chain risk.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
UMC developed its “Water Management Policy and Commitment” in 2015 to promote more
effective utilisation of water resources. In line with this policy, UMC continues to enhance
recycling through increased use of reclaimed and desalinated water, ongoing wastewater
treatment R&D, and optimised discharge management. In 2024, the company achieved a 30.3%
reduction in water withdrawal per unit product, surpassing its 14% goal and meeting its 2030
target ahead of schedule, which demonstrates UMC’s strong commitment to water conservation.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
2
To achieve the goal of zero pollution, UMC has established a comprehensive new material
evaluation process to effectively screen and control new materials introduced into the
development of new processes. Through this effort, it can avoid or reduce the use of raw
materials and the associated environmental impact. UMC was the first semiconductor
manufacturer company in the world to obtain QC-080000 IECQ Hazardous Substance Process
Management (HSPM) certification for all its fabs by leading global semiconductor manufacturers
in completing the third-party verification and audit of its HSPM system in 2006.
E GHG emissions 2
UMC was the first semiconductor company in Taiwan to pass integrated environmental
protection, safety and health management system certifications in 2001. UMC has established
the UMC Low Carbon Commitment as a guide for future carbon reduction projects. Its GHG
emission reduction goals include: 1) to reduce fluorinated greenhouse gas emissions intensity by
65% over 2013-25 vs. 2010, and 2) to reduce electricity consumption intensity by 15% in 10
years over 2016-25 vs. 2015.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing
Full speed on capacity expansion, backed by a
strong US commitment
Cloud AI demand strengthening, with no signs of slowing
TSMC management remains optimistic of a stronger multi-year Cloud AI mega-trend. Its
optimism is based on its prudent and comprehensive assessment of data centre
construction, power shell build up, component supply, and the financial situation of
Cloud service operators. Its capex target was retained at US$60-64bn in 2026, implying

a meaningful uptick to US$19.4-23.4bn in Q4 vs US$40.6bn in 9M26. Our recent
research highlighted TSMC is further accelerating expansion across N3/N2/A14, and
could lift capex to US$90bn in 2027E and US$105bn in 2028E.
Stronger commitment to  US expansion
TSMC guided the overseas dilution on gross margin to rise to 3-4% in 2027 from a
narrowing to 2% (vs. prior 2-3%) in 2026 given the accelerating expansion especially in
the US. The second fab in Arizona should start mass production for N3 in Q327, slightly

earlier than prior plan. Management also stated that it's in talk with SpaceX/Terafab on
potential collaboration, with key principle being to operate the fabs by TSMC.  As what
we wrote recently, TSMC is also evaluating the possibility to expand outside  Arizona,
with Texas being an option. The greater ambition to expand global footprint and
support reshoring is key in solidifying TSMC's competitive positioning, in our view.
Better Q4 sales guidance, slightly down GM guidance not concerning

Q3 EPS of NT$29.43 was above LSEG consensus of NT$28.67, mostly due to higher sales
and OPM of 58.7%. Q3 gross margin of 66.4% was a bit below expectation of 67%
into earnings, given N2 dilution. Q4 sales is guided to grow 14% QoQ at mid point,
slightly ahead of expectation of ~10% QoQ into earnings, driving full year sales
guidance in USD to grow 44% YoY. TSMC guided greater dilution from N2 and overseas
expansion, which lowers Q4 GM 40bps QoQ to 66.0% at the mid point of guidance. We

view TSMC’s investment case on strong growth at sustainable margins with good
support in 2027  (UBS GMs up 10bps YoY at 66.7% from 66.6% in 2026) with price
hikes to pass on higher costs.
Valuation: maintain Buy and target of NT$3,650
We raise 2026-28E EPS by 2-3% to reflect higher sales even with slightly lower gross
margin assumptions. Our new 2028E EPS is 10% above consensus pre-earnings. We
view resilient risk/reward for TSMC stock at 16x 2027E PE vs 36% earnings growth in

2027 based on UBSe, and expect the stock to steadily outperform the broader tech
sector at least through early 2027, assuming the market volatility persists. Reiterate Buy
rating. Our price target is based on 20x 2027-28E PE with a 26% long-term earnings
CAGR.

Market return assumption 6.3%
Forecast excess return 44.5%
Company Description
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSMC) is the world's leading dedicated foundry
service provider, with annual capacity of about 32m of 8-inch equivalent wafers as of end-
2024. It operates four 12-inch wafer fabs, four 8-inch wafer fabs and one 6-inch wafer fab in

Taiwan. TSMC also manages two 8-inch fabs and one 12-inch fab overseas under wholly
owned subsidiaries WaferTech in the US, and TSMC China and TSMC Nanjing in China.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We base our price target for TSMC on a PE multiple.
We think TSMC faces a variety of risks, including rapidly changing technology, intense
competition, high capital investment and cyclical demand. TSMC also has a history of paying
stock options to its employees, which could dilute earnings.

Quantitative Research Review
UBS Global Research publishes a quantitative assessment of its analysts' responses to certain questions about the likelihood of an occurrence
of a number of short term factors in a product known as the 'Quantitative Research Review'.  The views for this month can be found below.
Views contained in this assessment on a particular stock reflect only the views on those short term factors which are a different timeframe to

the 12-month timeframe reflected in any equity rating set out in this note.  For previous responses please make reference to (i) previous UBS
Global Research reports; and (ii) where no applicable research report was published that month, the Quantitative Research Review which can
be found at https://neo.ubs.com/quantitative, or contact your UBS sales representative for access to the report or the Quantitative Research
Team on ubs-quant-answers@ubs.com. A consolidated report which contains all responses is also available and again you should contact your

UBS sales representative for details and pricing or the Quantitative Research Team on the email above.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

(ESG) factors into investment process and portfolio construction. Strategies across geographies approach ESG analysis and incorporate the findings in a variety of ways.
Incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing considerations may inhibit the portfolio manager’s ability to participate in certain investment opportunities that otherwise

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

Earnings revision: We factor in WNC 2Q26 result and revise up earnings by 1%/ 2%/ 2% 
in 2026-28E to reflect higher than expected revenues of WiFi 7/ WiFi 8 and switch 
products, driven by spec upgrade and rising client demand. We keep GM and Opex ratio 
largely unchanged.

Valuation: With the earning estimate changes, we raise our 12M TP to NT$380.0 (vs. 
previously at NT$371, based on 28.0x 2027E P/E, unchanged). We continue to base 
our TP on 2027E P/E, and the target multiple is still derived from the correlation 
between P/E and EPS growth of its peers, which is higher as we mark to market. Our 
target P/E of 28.0x is higher than company’s avg. +1 std P/E (16.3x), which we believe is 
not stretched considering the sustainable growth of WNC driven by long-term satellite 
and high speed networking demand. Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology: WNC 
Valuation: We use a near-term P/E to derive our 12m target price for WNC, consistent 
with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our TP of NT$380.0 on a target P/E 
multiple of 28.0x on a forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E is derived from the 
correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers. We are Buy rated on WNC. Key 
downside risks: fiercer-than-expected competition in satellite communication, 
slower-than-expected Wi-Fi 7 or 5G FWA ramp-up in US and Europe markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

Issue & News 
SK하이닉스  
(000660) 
 소음보다 방향성에 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 3,200,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.09.23) 
1,862,000 
반도체업종

◼ 최근 시장은 2가지 Risk를 제기. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 
◼ 현 주가는 보다 강인해지는 Fundamental의 가치를 미반영한 수준. 
◼ 비중 확대 전략 유효. 목표주가와 매수의견 유지.

투자전략: 업황을 대변하지 못하고 있는 주가 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 현 주가는 업황의 강세를 충분히 반영하지 못하
고 있다고 생각. 1) 메모리반도체 사이클의 추가 확장 (구매 경쟁 심화에 따른 
단기 가격 상승 폭 확대, 기대치를 상회할 4Q26 DRAM 판매량), 2) 3Q26 실
적발표에서 보다 구체화될 주주환원 정책의 방향성에 보다 집중할 필요. 
시장에서 제기하는 Risk가 있다면, 2가지. 1) 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈, 
2) Solidigm IPO와 해외 공장 투자에 따른 경계심리 확대가 그 대상. 당사는 
해당 Risk가 Fundamental을 저해할 것으로 보고 있지 않으며, 단기 노이즈보
다는 사이클 확장의 선순환에 보다 주목할 필요가 있다고 생각. 
1. HBM4 품질 이슈, 과도한 우려는 기우 
3Q26부터 HBM4 출하가 본격화될 것이라는 기존 입장을 유지. HBM4 비중
은 3Q26 전체 판매량의 40%, 2027년 연간 50% 중반까지 상승할 것. 
일각에서는 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈로 사업 성과가 기대치를 하회할 
수 있다는 우려를 표출. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 후공정 단에서 문제가 일
부 발생했을 수 있으나, 해결 불가능한 Risk라 보기 어렵고, 불량에 따른 배
상금을 논할 단계도 아니라는 판단. 
2. 해외 공장 투자, 실보다 득이 크다 
언론보도에 따르면, SK하이닉스는 해외 공장 투자와 Solidigm IPO를 추진 중. 
해당 결정이 현실화될 경우, 시장은 단기적으로 이를 부정적으로 받아들일 
가능성이 존재. 다만, 결과적으로, 실보다 득이 큰 선택이 될 것이라 생각. 
- 해외 투자가 현실화되더라도, 실질 생산 기여 시점은 2028년 이후일 가능
성이 높음. 미국의 건설 경기와 공장 구축의 세부 단계 등을 감안할 필요. 
- 해외 투자 결정이 관세 Risk를 축소시킬 수 있는 변화라는 점도 감안할 필
요. “불확실성 해소”는 Upcycle의 중반부에서 반도체 주식에 대한 좋은 투자
포인트가 되어왔고, 이번에도 다르지 않을 것이라는 판단.

Document 230: AI发现PHP严重漏洞！全球75%网站面临威胁，欧洲多国政府网站紧急下线

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI发现PHP严重漏洞！全球75%网站面临威胁，欧洲多国政府网站紧急下线.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

美国国家安全局周三发出紧急警告，研究⼈员借助⾕歌和 OpenAI 旗下 AI ⼯具在 PHP 编程语
⾔中发现了两处严重安全漏洞，全球⼤量⽹站因此⾯临被⿊客⼊侵的重⼤⻛险。荷兰、法
国、丹⻨、芬兰等多国政府⽹站紧急下线。 PHP 为全球约 75% ⽹站提供⽀撑， OpenAI 认为
这是近期影响最深远的安全问题之⼀。
摘要：
全球互联⽹基础设施⾯临⾃⼈⼯智能⼯具普及以来最严峻的安全威胁之⼀。
美国国家安全局（ NSA ）周三发出紧急警告，研究⼈员借助 AI ⼯具在 PHP 编程语⾔中发现了两
处严重安全漏洞，全球⼤量⽹站因此⾯临被⿊客⼊侵的重⼤⻛险。荷兰、法国、丹⻨、芬兰等
多国政府⽹站紧急下线。
PHP 开发团队于周三发布紧急补丁，并呼吁所有⽤⼾ " ⽴即更新 " 。该漏洞在 PHP 代码库中潜伏逾
⼗年，此次由美国⽹络安全公司借助⾕歌和 OpenAI 旗下 AI ⼯具发现。
由于补丁已公开发布，专家警告称，⿊客可利⽤ AI 轻易对漏洞进⾏逆向⼯程，⼤规模⼊侵攻击
浪潮或⼀触即发。
据互联⽹情报公司 Censys 的数据，在约 260 万个运⾏ PHP 的主机中，⽬前约有 150 万个使⽤的版

本尚⽆可⽤补丁。PHP 为全球约 75% 已知编程语⾔构成的⽹站提供底层⽀撑， WordPress 、
Drupal 、 Joomla 和 Magento 均建⽴于这⼀技术之上，任何关键漏洞的连锁影响极为深远。
OpenAI 周三表⽰， " 鉴于这些漏洞的严重程度以及 PHP 的⼴泛应⽤，我们认为这是近期记忆中影
响最深远的安全问题之⼀" ，并强调 " 据我们所知，前沿 AI 在攻击者之前率先发现了这些漏洞 " 。
AI 发现 PHP 漏洞，此前已存在逾⼗年
此次漏洞由 AI ⼯具率先发现，标志着⽹络安全攻防态势出现结构性转变。⾕歌旗下⽹络安全公司
Wiz 在博客中披露，其⾃主 AI 漏洞研究⼯具 Wiz Atlas 结合⾕歌 Gemini 4 Argon 及 OpenAI 的 GPT-
5.6 Sol 发现了上述缺陷。
另⼀家⽹络安全公司 Calif 亦表⽰，其于 8 ⽉ 1 ⽇借助⼤语⾔模型独⽴发现同⼀漏洞，此后持续参
与修复⼯作。两家公司均称已相互协调，并与 PHP 团队及 OpenAI 共同跟进处置。

Wiz 指出，上述漏洞在 PHP 代码库中隐匿逾⼗年，若依赖⼈⼯排查，其利⽤难度极⾼。" 在 AI 出
现之前，这⼀⼿⼯⼯作实施困难，更难以规模化推进， "Wiz 表⽰， " 有了 AI ，这⼀障碍已不复存
在。 "
⽹络安全研究与事件响应公司 TLP:Black 联合创始⼈ Costin Raiu 警告称， AI 正在驱动⼀场 " 争先
利⽤的竞赛 " ，下⼀步将是 " 互联⽹上所有存在漏洞的⽹络服务器遭到⼤规模攻陷 " 。
七成⽹站受威胁，技术资源匮乏者⾸当其冲
PHP 诞⽣于 1990 年代初，最初代表 "Personal Home Page" ，如今已成为互联⽹公共端最重要的
底层语⾔之⼀。根据⽹络技术追踪机构 W3Techs 的数据， PHP 约为 75% 已知编程语⾔构成的⽹
站提供运⾏⽀撑。

Drupal 协会临时⾸席执⾏官 Tiffany Farriss 表⽰： " 所有使⽤过去 20 年间任何 PHP 版本的服务器都
将⾯临漏洞⻛险，这⼀问题影响互联⽹的⼤多数站点。 "
WordPress.com 运营⽅ Automattic 已表⽰，其托管服务⽤⼾及 Tumblr ⽤⼾不受此次漏洞影响。
但⻛险集中指向规模较⼩的 PHP 驱动⽹站 —— 尤其是由个⼈或机构运营、技术资源有限、依赖
共享主机服务且⽆法直接管理底层服务器的站点。
伦敦国王学院访问⾼级研究员 Lukasz Olejnik 警告称， " 规模巨⼤，加之 AI 的介⼊，攻击可能即刻
发⽣。 "
多国政府紧急响应，下线浪潮席卷欧洲
欧洲政府机构率先采取预防措施，多国⽹站相继下线。
荷兰国家⽹络安全中⼼（ NCSC ）⾃⾝⽹站亦关闭下线，并将此举定性为 " 预防措施 " 。⿅特丹
港、政府⻔⼾⽹站 Overheid.nl 、荷兰⾜球协会 KNVB 、该国五⼤政党官⽹及多家医院⽹站均跟进
暂停服务。荷兰⾸相表⽰正密切关注事态发展。
芬兰国家 IT 服务提供商 Valtori 宣布，政府公共⽹络服务已作为 " 预防措施 " 下线。法国内政部表
⽰，已将旗下所有⽹站置于 " 预防性维护 " 状态。丹⻨市政 IT 公司 Kombit 称已关闭多项服务，包括
全国学校通讯平台。
⽹络百科全书维基百科称已下线数量未披露的系统，等待安全补丁完成部署；线上图书馆互联
⽹档案馆（ Internet Archive ）亦表⽰曾短暂下线以处理该问题。

Document 265: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

Supply still hyper

### Page 2

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

2 
Summary 
• We are set for another heavy year of supply across currencies and products in 2027, 
although volumes may ease slightly from 2026 in many areas. 
• Hyperscalers and the wider tech sector remain major drivers of issuance and feature 
prominently across our forecasts. 
• EUR corporate supply is forecast at €444bn, easing modestly from 2026 amid higher 
rates, wider spreads, hyperscaler fatigue and a more volatile backdrop. 
• TMT and utilities capex should continue to drive EUR corporate supply. 
• Bank bond supply will remain high, driven largely by redemptions, while the modest 
macro outlook is unlikely to push issuance much higher. 
• USD corporate issuance is set for another hefty year, with supply forecast at around 
$1tr, driven by hyperscalers. 
• Hyperscalers will also drive Reverse Yankee supply, bringing significant volumes to 
the EUR market despite less attractive economics. 
• Higher debt costs will weigh slightly on hybrid issuance, but volumes should remain 
elevated, supported by utilities capex, rating defence, active M&A and Moody’s new 
structure. 
• ESG supply is set for another strong year.

### Page 3

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

3

We expect corporate supply to fall back to €444bn in 2027, around 8.5% lower than 2026's record level

It's all the 4s... again 
2026 is set to be a record year for euro supply at €485bn. Elevated redemptions, capex, 
M&A, ALM activity, hybrid issuance and, of course, the heavy reverse Yankee supply, 
particularly from US tech issuers, have all supported issuance volumes. 
Looking to 2027, many of these drivers remain in place, but higher rates, slightly wider 
spreads, steeper curves, hyperscaler fatigue and a more volatile backdrop should see 
supply slow modestly from record highs. 
As such, we expect supply to fall back to €444bn in 2027, approximately 8.5% lower 
than 2026's record level. That would bring issuance back in line with the €444bn seen in 
2020 and close to the €455bn printed in 2025. More importantly, it would mark the first 
decline in net supply in several years, providing a more supportive technical backdrop for 
credit spreads. Net supply is pencilled in at €126bn after redemptions rise up to €318bn. 
Corporate supply to fall to €444bn

Source: ING, Dealogic

Timothy Rahill 
Credit Strategist 
timothy.rahill@ing.com

Jeroen van den Broek 
Global Head of Sector Research 
jeroen.van.den.broek@ing.com

Corporate supply: a return to €444bn 
Higher rates, wider spreads, steeper curves, hyperscaler fatigue and a more volatile 
backdrop should see corporate supply ease modestly from record highs. We forecast 
€444bn of supply in 2027

### Page 4

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

4 
Higher rates, higher cost of debt 
All-in yields in EUR IG credit have risen back towards 4.5%, driven largely by higher 
underlying rates rather than wider credit spreads. 
Looking ahead, our rates strategists expect a modest steepening bias in 2027, with slightly 
more downward momentum at the front end of the curve. The broader message, 
however, is that rates should remain elevated enough to keep all-in yields attractive. 
We expect only limited changes in outright rates, curve shape and swap spreads, leaving 
all-in yields broadly intact. As a result, the search for yield should remain a powerful 
tailwind for credit markets throughout 2027, although the same elevated rates continue 
to increase the cost of debt for issuers. 
All-in yield for credit rises

Source: ING, IHS Markit

Capex and M&A are on the rise 
Utilities capex will remain a key driver of issuance, with the sector set for another heavy 
funding year as we forecast around €75bn of supply in 2027. M&A activity should also 
continue to support primary market volumes. While technology has grabbed the 
headlines, M&A outside the sector has also accelerated, with both US and European deal 
activity reaching new highs in 2026. 
The pipeline remains healthy, suggesting momentum should carry into at least the first 
half of 2027. However, higher rates, rising debt costs and somewhat tighter liquidity 
conditions are likely to become a greater constraint over time. As a result, we expect 
M&A-related issuance to remain supportive of supply, albeit at a more moderate pace as 
the year progresses. 
M&A activity reaching new highs

Source: ING

It's all about utilities and TMT 
Across sectors, issuance should broadly converge towards redemption needs. Utilities

sectors and the influence from Corporate Hybrids and Reverse Yankee issuance later in 
this report.

### Page 6

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

6

High redemption volumes are expected to keep EUR bank bond supply elevated in 2027

Variables driving next year's bank bond supply 
We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027, down slightly from the 
€455bn forecast for 2026. In our view, several variables will be steering next year’s 
issuances. 
Redemptions will remain the central driver for the elevated 2027 bank bond supply 
forecast. Nearly €400bn of bonds mature in 2027, up €30bn year-on-year and the 
highest level in a decade. The increase is especially significant in the covered bond 
segment where €186bn will mature, a historical high. 
The subordinated segment will also rise, with redemptions totalling €45bn, up about 
€6bn from 2026. Of this total, €31bn is in Tier 2 instruments and €14bn in AT1s. The 
senior unsecured segment will partially offset these increases, with total redemptions 
reaching more than €162bn, down from more than €170bn recorded this year. The 
majority stems from senior bail-in bonds (senior non-preferred or senior HoldCo) with 
more than €90bn and an additional €71bn maturing in senior preferred instruments. 
Despite diverging trends across segments, elevated redemption volumes remain the 
main driver of bank issuance in the euro bond primary market, as institutions refinance 
maturing instruments. 
Bank bond supply stays cruising at 
altitude in 2027 
We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027. Redemptions remain the 
main driver of the high supply, but the modest macroeconomic outlook is not expected 
to push supply up any further. We foresee covered bond issuance reaching €185bn, 
senior unsecured bond supply at €210bn and another €55bn in subordinated bank bonds

Marine Leleux 
Sector Strategist, Financials 
marine.leleux2@ing.com

Jesse Norcross 
Senior Sector Strategist, Real Estate 
jesse.norcross@ing.com

### Page 7

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

7 
Bank bond redemptions per segment

Source: ING

At first glance, high redemption levels point to stronger supply in 2027. However, other 
factors play a role, such as the macroeconomic environment. Eurozone GDP growth is 
forecast to increase slightly in the second half of next year to reach 1.5%, still well below 
levels seen after the pandemic. 
Furthermore, our economists pencil in an additional ECB rate hike in 2026, bringing 
policy rates to 2.75% in early 2027. The higher interest rate environment is not expected 
to positively impact lending growth, especially as geopolitical instability remains. As a 
result, we do not see the modest macroeconomic backdrop acting as a catalyst for bank 
bond supply in 2027. 
Policy rates and GDP growth forecasts

Source: ING

Asset swap spread levels initially proved very resilient to geopolitical instability and 
related economic changes this year. Despite some volatility in March, following the 
outbreak of the US-Israel conflict with Iran, spread levels recovered quickly and reached 
levels close to their historical low. However, a broader escalation of the conflict across 
the Middle East, coupled with the absence of a clear path to resolution, could put further 
pressure on spreads. 
In addition, budget and political concerns in France and OAT-Bund volatility, as well as 
broader European fiscal concerns, have recently led to spreads widening again. The 
combination of French political uncertainty and the Middle East conflict could trigger 
some periods of volatility in the market in 2027. However, we believe these uncertainties 
will not be a strong negative for bank bond supply. Depending on the timing, though,

### Page 8

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

8 
periodic widening may make financial institutions less keen to pre-fund redemptions 
coming up in 2028. 
Our 2027 supply forecast 
When accounting for these three main variables, we expect the EUR bank bond supply 
to reach €450bn in 2027. This translates into a net supply of €57bn, down from €89bn in 
2026. 
Forecasted EUR bank bond supply for 2027

YTD = 16/10 
Source: ING

Our estimate includes €185bn in covered bonds, comprising €180bn in benchmark-sized 
issuances, including floating rate notes, and €5bn in sub-benchmark bonds. This is a 
€5bn drop versus our 2026 forecast. It reflects the pre-financing already made by issuers 
this year in preparation for the historically high redemptions. 
Furthermore, next year’s mortgage lending forecasts point to stagnation, somewhat 
muting any further increase in covered bond issuances. Besides, with the securitisation 
package entering its finalisation stage, issuers with high funding needs may shift some 
secured issuance towards securitisation for diversification purposes. Increasing demand 
for covered bonds from overseas investors driven by regulatory developments may also 
result in more currency diversification, away from EUR-denominated issuance. 
We forecast senior unsecured supply to reach €210bn next year. This is split between 
€70bn in senior preferred bonds and €140bn in senior bail-in instruments. It represents a 
€10bn YoY decline in senior preferred supply, reflecting both the drop in the segment’s 
redemptions from 2027 onwards and the potential impact of the Crisis Management 
and Deposit Insurance (CMDI) framework. As the CMDI reforms are expected to enter 
into force in 2028, they could reduce the attractiveness of senior preferred bonds versus 
senior bail-in instruments in some EU countries, as senior preferred exposures will rank 
lower than, and no longer pari passu to, uncovered deposits in the creditor hierarchy. 
The funding cost advantage of senior preferred unsecured issuance could decline. At the 
same time, issuing senior non-preferred bonds could serve the purpose of increasing the 
bail-inable layer protecting preferred senior bonds and their current ratings. This could 
shift some issuers’ focus towards the senior non-preferred bucket instead of the senior 
preferred segment. 
Our forecast for senior non-preferred supply remains stable compared to our 2026 
estimate. While redemptions will increase by €6bn in 2027 and further in 2028 to reach 
€102bn, we believe the impact of higher refinancing needs on non-preferred senior 
supply will be partially offset by the lack of balance sheet growth against the backdrop 
of higher interest rates. Furthermore, the outperformance of US dollar debt versus EUR-

### Page 9

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

9 
denominated debt could see issuers turn more often towards the USD market, 
potentially negatively affecting EUR senior bail-in supply. 
Lastly, we expect Tier 2 supply to reach €35bn and AT1 supply to reach €20bn in 2027. 
This is a €5bn increase YoY in both AT1 and Tier 2 compared to our 2026 forecast, mainly 
driven by higher redemption levels. With AT1 redemptions in 2027 increasing by €3bn 
YoY to €14bn, net supply is likely to be marginally higher next year. In Tier 2, 
redemptions increase by €4bn to €31bn, essentially meaning net supply will only be 
marginally higher. 
As the chart below highlights, net supply in AT1 has been significant in recent years but 
has slowed this year as the focus has shifted towards refinancing. As such, while we 
expect more AT1 supply next year compared to 2026, the levels are relatively in line 
with the longer-term trend. 
Forecast EUR AT1 supply (€bn)

Source: ING

The main driver of supply will also be refinancing for subordinated capital. We expect a 
further marginal decline in CET1 buffers, although most banks are already operating at 
target AT1 and Tier 2 ratios. Still, there are some issuers with substantial gaps in either 
AT1 or Tier 2 ratios, which they may look to improve, leading to a slight increase in 
supply. 
Significant Risk Transfer (SRT) issuance has also increased in recent years and will 
continue to grow as a tool for banks in terms of balance sheet and capital management, 
while it is also growing as an investable asset class. Although SRTs provide capital and 
RWA relief, they do not replace the gone-concern and loss absorption capacity of AT1 
and Tier 2 instruments. As such, it plays a more 'complementary' role in capital 
management, and we do not expect it to have a major impact on bank capital issuance 
in 2027. 
Bank M&A could also be a wildcard for 2027 issuance. With several large M&A 
transactions potentially taking place, this may lead to some capital optimisation and 
less incremental supply needs; conversely, goodwill effects, potential regulatory 
increases in buffer requirements or RWA inflation may lead to a slight increase in the 
need for capital. However, on the whole, we would expect M&A to have a slight 
dampener effect on supply. 
Finally, spreads are currently quite elevated, and coupons on new issues are generally 
higher than legacy bonds maturing or callable in 2027. This may also lead to some more 
wait-and-see from issuers, but we still think that most issuers will continue to drive their 
capital plans as in previous years.

Dollar supply stays strong as corporate issuance heads towards another US$1tr year

We forecast another hefty year for USD corporate issuance in 2027, with gross supply 
again expected to be around US$1tr. While below the record-like US$1.1tr seen in both 
2020 and 2026, issuance is expected to remain exceptionally strong by historical 
standards. 
Redemptions are expected to remain broadly unchanged at US$553bn, leaving net 
supply of around US$447bn. While this is a modest improvement from 2026, the USD 
market will continue to face a significantly heavier net supply burden than its EUR 
counterpart. 
USD Corporate supply set to see another big year

Source: ING, Dealogic

Hyperscalers and tech issuers are a driving force 
As discussed above, the scale of investment required for data centres, energy 
infrastructure and digital capacity is unprecedented. The financing burden has spread 
USD supply: Another trillion for 
corporates 
We forecast another hefty year for USD corporate issuance in 2027, with supply once 
again set to reach around US$1tr

well beyond technology firms themselves and is increasingly influencing broader capital 
markets. 
Already in 2026, the hyperscalers have been a major source of debt issuance, raising 
around US$237bn across currencies, including US$160bn in USD and US$31bn in EUR. 
Issuance has been led by the usual suspects: Amazon, Alphabet, Meta, Oracle and IBM. 
Looking ahead, we expect hyperscaler funding needs to increase further as investment 
in AI infrastructure, data centres and power capacity accelerates. As a result, we 
forecast total issuance of around US$280bn in 2027, including approximately US$190bn 
in USD and US$65bn in EUR (€60bn). 
The rest of the technology sector will also remain very busy in 2027, with an expected 
US$200bn forecast to come to market. 
M&A is substantial 
M&A activity should continue to support primary market volumes. While technology has 
grabbed the headlines, M&A outside the sector has also accelerated, with both US and 
European deal activity reaching new highs in 2026. The pipeline remains healthy, 
suggesting momentum should carry into at least the first half of 2027. 
However, higher rates, rising debt costs and somewhat tighter liquidity conditions are 
likely to become a greater constraint over time. As a result, we expect M&A-related 
issuance to remain supportive of supply, albeit at a more moderate pace as the year 
progresses. 
Higher rates, higher cost of debt 
All-in yields in USD IG credit have risen back towards 5.2% (5yr), driven largely by higher 
underlying rates rather than wider credit spreads. 
Looking ahead, our rates strategists expect a modest steepening bias in 2027, with 
slightly more downward momentum at the front end of the curve. The broader 
message, however, is that rates should remain elevated enough to keep all-in yields 
attractive. 
We expect only limited changes in outright rates, curve shape and swap spreads, leaving 
all-in yields broadly intact. As a result, the search for yield should remain a powerful 
tailwind for credit markets throughout 2027, although the same elevated rates continue 
to increase the cost of debt for issuers.

Sectors supply shows the amount of tech issuance 
Most sectors are expected to see relatively modest net supply in 2027. The clear 
exception is TMT, which will once again dominate issuance volumes. We forecast around 
US$180bn of supply from the hyperscalers alone, with a further US$850bn coming from 
the broader technology universe. Telecoms should add another US$60bn, leaving TMT 
firmly at the centre of the supply story. 
Utilities remain another major source of issuance as rising capex requirements continue 
to drive funding needs, extending a trend that has defined the sector in recent years. 
Healthcare and Industrials should also remain active issuers, although a sizeable portion 
of gross supply will be offset by elevated redemption profiles. 
In short, while net supply remains contained across much of the market, technology and 
utilities continue to shoulder the bulk of issuance growth in 2027.

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

14

We expect hyperscalers alone to account for around €60bn of Reverse Yankee issuance in 2027

The key question for 2027 is whether Reverse Yankee supply can remain as convincing 
as it has been in recent years. The economics for US issuers looking to fund in euros and 
swap proceeds back into dollars have deteriorated markedly, with much of the historical 
funding advantage having evaporated. In many cases, the maths is now far less 
compelling on an issuer-by-issuer basis. 
However, the hyperscaler story remains difficult to ignore. We expect hyperscalers alone 
to account for around €60bn of Reverse Yankee issuance in 2027, up sharply from the 
roughly €31bn issued in euros during 2026. 
As a result, we continue to expect elevated Reverse Yankee supply, albeit increasingly 
concentrated within the technology sector. Overall, we forecast another €100bn of 
Reverse Yankee issuance in 2027, matching that of 2026.

€100bn 
Reverse Yankee supply forecast for 2027

Importantly, this leaves only around €40bn of issuance from non-hyperscaler borrowers, 
a notable decline on what we saw in 2026. The decline reflects the reduced cost-saving 
incentive for US corporates to issue in euros and swap back to dollars. 
Reverse Yankee supply will come 
mostly from hyperscalers 
2027 is set to be another heavy year for issuance across currencies and products, even if 
supply moderates somewhat from 2026 levels in many segments. Hyperscalers and the 
wider technology sector will remain key drivers of funding activity and are among the 
largest contributors to our overall supply forecasts

This also suggests that continued strength in overall corporate funding volumes should 
still provide a supportive backdrop, even if the currency economics become less 
attractive.

16

Hybrid supply is set to come off its record highs, but remains elevated

Corporate hybrid supply should remain reasonably elevated in 2027, although below 
recent peaks. First call volumes will fall to just €23bn. However, some hybrid first calls 
scheduled for 1H27 have yet to be refinanced, implying true refinancing needs are 
closer to €19bn for 2027. 
The same goes for 2028, where a good proportion of 1H28 calls will be refinanced in 
(late) 2027; however, this number is limited in volume as we see a further drop in first 
calls in 2028 at just €19bn (€12bn in 1H), so we estimate that half of those will be 
refinanced early, thus adding €6bn to the refinancing need in 2027. This takes the total 
refinancing need to €25bn. 
However, the following should continue to support issuance as in 2026, and we estimate 
this will add another €10bn. Making up our forecast of €35bn, is: 
• Rising utility capex requirements. 
• Rating-defence considerations. 
• A still-active M&A backdrop. 
• And the new 30NCs Moody's structure. 
Hybrid supply fades, but will stay 
elevated 
Corporate hybrid supply should remain reasonably elevated in 2027, although below 
recent peaks. We forecast €35bn in corporate hybrid supply next year

### Page 17

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

17 
Hybrid supply vs first-call dates

Source: Dealogic

On the hybrid structure front, we remain comfortable with both the methodology and 
the underlying structures. The new Moody's 30NC format has also proved popular, 
accounting for €15bn of issuance in 2026. The fact that the Moody's 30NC structure is 
just one notch down from the Basic Credit Assessment, rather than the usual two 
notches, results in IFRS debt accounting rather than IFRS equity accounting. 
In 2026, issuance in this format reached €15bn, accounting for 32% of the €46bn 
market, with three issuers managing to attach an investment-grade rating to this 
format rather than under the traditional boilerplate format. We are confident that this 
30NC structure has helped supply hit its 2026 dizzying heights. 
The main downside risk to our €35bn forecast is that higher rates continue to raise the 
cost of hybrid funding, although the headwind is materially less severe than in 2022-23. 
Combined with slightly wider spreads and a more volatile market backdrop, issuance 
could moderate further from the above.

### Page 18

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

18

AI and data centre investment is expected to be the main driver of TMT bond issuance in 2027

We expect another year of strong supply for the European TMT sector. We expect 
€150bn of gross issuance and €103bn of net issuance, given €47bn of debt redemptions. 
The additional issuance is not coming from the traditional telecom or media issuers, but 
from global tech companies. 
As we expect the data centre build-out to continue in 2027, issuers are likely to return to 
the European investment-grade credit market to raise part of the required funding. 
Meanwhile, we do not see a major reason for traditional sectors to reduce or increase 
indebtedness, as factors driving indebtedness higher are countered by factors driving 
debt lower, as we’ll explain below.

### Page 19

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

19 
Telecoms supply 
We will break down the main factors affecting debt issuance, starting with FY27 capex 
requirements. As a percentage of sales, we expect capex to decline in FY27. However, 
companies such as Orange and Vodafone are integrating recent acquisitions and may 
need to invest additional capital to realise merger synergies. Overall, we expect 
aggregate capex to increase slightly because of the impact of consolidation. We, 
therefore, do not expect lower capex to result in less issuance. Nevertheless, the 
companies BT and KPN are exceptions, as both clearly intend to reduce capex in FY27. 
We also do not expect EBITDA growth to drive materially higher indebtedness. Low-
single-digit EBITDA growth gives companies some capacity to issue additional debt while 
maintaining stable leverage. However, higher interest rates incentivise reducing relative 
indebtedness. Instead, we expect the increase in debt issuance to be driven mainly by 
M&A. Several debt-funded acquisitions of smaller networks could generate around €6bn 
of net issuance. A further €4bn could come from US operators raising euro-denominated 
funding for spectrum payments in 2027. 
Technology supply 
In 2027, we believe the AI build-out will continue. Tech companies currently report 
healthy returns on investment, demand for AI services continues to outpace supply, and 
companies want to maintain their market positions. We therefore expect further capex 
growth in 2027, with the large hyperscalers investing close to US$1tr in fiscal 2027. 
Like in 2026, we expect these companies to finance their investments largely from 
operating cash flow, but also through debt issuance, with additional funding coming 
from European markets. We expect hyperscale data centre operators to issue at least 
€60bn in 2027, somewhat above the approximately €35bn issued year-to-date in 2026, 
including issuance by NTT. 
In addition to the €60bn of issuance from hyperscale data centre operators, we expect 
€38bn of net supply from other technology issuers. Companies are likely to pursue 
acquisitions and raise additional funding for investment, including semiconductor, 
software and payment services companies. Including €10bn from telecom issuers, this 
brings total TMT net supply to €103bn. This would translate into gross supply of 
approximately €150bn, which, in our view, is close to the amount the European market 
may be able to absorb. 
Supply discipline will be important 
Companies will need to issue prudently and provide clear guidance on future funding 
plans, as spreads may only tighten once the market no longer expects substantial new 
supply. Nevertheless, current spreads look attractive relative to the issuers’ high ratings, 
with much of the widening driven by supply and liquidity effects. Relatively wide CDS 
spreads also suggest that technical factors, rather than a material deterioration in credit 
risk, are weighing on valuations. This combination of high credit quality and attractive 
spreads should attract new money to the investment-grade market. 
Challenge 1: Does debt issuance reduce credit quality? 
First, we expect the hyperscale data centre operators Amazon, Microsoft, Alphabet, Meta 
and Oracle to grow their combined EBITDA by US$230bn, or 29%, in FY27, to around 
US$1tr. Moreover, with the exception of Oracle, these companies are expected to 
operate with limited net debt, with Microsoft holding more cash than debt. The main 
caveat is their significant lease commitments, many of which are not yet reflected in 
balance-sheet lease liabilities because the underlying leases have not commenced. 
Given our aggregate EBITDA projection of around US$1tr for fiscal 2027, alongside 
generally strong balance sheets and operating cash-flow generation, we are not yet 
concerned that these commitments materially weaken credit quality.

### Page 20

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

20 
We prefer to wait for the 10-K filings before assessing the full impact, as annual reports 
provide more comprehensive lease and contractual-commitment disclosures. Over time, 
there is a risk that rating agencies will attach greater weight to these liabilities, 
particularly if returns on investment start to decline. In our view, this is not a material 
concern for 2027 and may never become one, given the sector’s strong growth and 
ability to fund a significant part of investment from operating cash flow. Companies also 
retain flexibility to scale back future investment that has not yet been committed if 
returns start to disappoint. 
Challenge 2: Do credit benchmarks accommodate more supply? 
Do European credit markets have the capacity to absorb additional supply from 
technology companies? We think the current share of technology issuers remains 
modest. Alphabet accounts for only 1.16% of the iBoxx € Non-Financials index, while 
Amazon and IBM account for 0.68% and 0.91%, respectively. 
In the US, Oracle’s share of more than 2% in the iBoxx $ Domestic Non-Financials index 
illustrates that individual technology issuers can reach materially higher benchmark 
weights, while several high-yield indices apply a 3% issuer cap. In addition, many large 
technology companies have not yet raised funding from European credit markets. 
We therefore see significant room for technology companies to increase issuance in 
European investment-grade markets. The inclusion of eligible new bonds in benchmarks 
also creates a strong technical incentive for benchmark-aware investors to participate in 
new deals, as not owning the bonds could increase their tracking error.

We expect high bond supply in 2027 for utilities due to redemptions and high capital expenditures

We forecast European utilities to raise €75bn via bond markets in 
2027 
2027 is likely to be another eventful year for European utilities. Geopolitical uncertainty, 
volatility in energy markets and significant investment pipelines, together with the 
associated funding needs, should remain key drivers for the sector going into next year. 
We forecast €75bn of total bond supply from investment-grade utilities in 2027, only 
slightly below the €80bn we have pencilled in for full-year 2026. Net supply also remains 
firmly positive at €35bn next year, although somewhat below the €44bn in 2026.

Corporate hybrids remain an important part of the funding mix 
Utilities have raised €19bn with corporate hybrids so far in 2026, the highest level since 
2015. Hybrid issuance helps protect the credit profiles of these companies as they 
finance their extensive investment pipelines with debt alongside cash flows. 
While we expect corporate hybrids to remain an important part of the funding mix of 
utilities in 2027, the choice between senior and hybrid funding will always reflect market 
conditions and the relative pricing of the two instrument classes. 
Utilities hybrid supply at record highs in 2026 
Euro-denominated bond issuance from investment grade utilities

Source: ING, Dealogic

High capital expenditure drives supply 
Alongside redemptions, bond supply continues to be driven by large capital expenditure. 
We expect combined investment to top €200bn next year for large European utility 
issuers. German names have the highest investment needs for 2027, up around 30% 
year-on-year, while Italian, UK, and Dutch names are also among those seeing a clear 
increase. 
The energy transition continues to drive significant investment needs, with growing 
electrification and renewable generation requiring substantial expansion and 
reinforcement of transmission and distribution grids. Regulated utilities are particularly 
impacted by higher grid investments, but integrated utilities are also likely to see higher 
capital expenditure next year. 
Higher interest rates may make some companies more selective in their investments. 
The bulk of these programmes is likely to proceed, however, as the acute investment 
needs of many network utilities are unlikely to change any time soon. Potential 
execution constraints could delay the timing of some capex, but we do not expect this to 
have a major impact on overall bond supply.

Credit Supply Outlook 2027 October 2026

24

Sustainable bank bond supply is blooming, nearing a record in 2026

Banks issued an unexpectedly large amount of sustainable debt this year despite 
Europe’s ESG deregulation efforts. Up to mid-October 2026, they issued more than 
€86bn in sustainable bonds across different instrument types, up by almost €20bn from 
the same period last year. 
This was a surprisingly strong increase given that favourable primary market conditions 
offered issuers little greenium, despite continued evidence that sustainable bonds 
attract higher investor demand, as reflected in stronger oversubscription rates. This 
suggests that the availability of sustainable assets was not a meaningful constraint on 
banks' sustainable debt issuance, thanks to higher redemptions and sustainable lending 
growth. 
Issuers are also increasingly directing ESG bond issuance towards European capital 
markets, often at the expense of North American markets, reflecting the growing 
divergence in political and regulatory priorities between the two regions. 
The bulk of the YTD supply (€76.6bn) (re)financed green assets, with green supply up 
€25bn from 2025. Instead, issuance in social and sustainability formats did not exceed 
€9.6bn, trailing last year’s supply by €4.5bn. All instrument types, from secured to 
unsecured to subordinated, contributed to the higher ESG supply volumes. However, 
sustainable T2 and bail-in senior (senior non-preferred and HoldCo) issuance increased 
particularly strongly, as the funding cost advantage from sustainable issuance was 
better for these instruments than for covered bonds and preferred senior debt. 
Issuance under the European Green Bond Standard (EuGBS) rose from €2.5bn to almost 
€12bn YTD. For many banks, ICMA-aligned green bond issuance remained the more 
practical option as it allows them to finance a broader pool of assets, rather than fully 
commit the bond proceeds to Taxonomy-aligned activities. This is particularly relevant

simplification agenda.

What is driving growth in ESG supply in 2027? 
We expect sustainable bank bond supply to reach €100bn in FY2027, up from €93bn in 
FY2026 and equivalent to 22% of total euro-denominated bank bond supply. Green 
issuance remains dominant at €85bn, of which €17bn (20%) will be issued under the EU 
Green Bond Standard (EuGBS). Social, sustainable and sustainability-linked supply will 
total €15bn, of which 80% will be issued in social format. 
Greater availability of social assets, together with regulatory initiatives such as the 
Affordable Housing Act presented by the European Commission in September 2026, 
should help social issuance to gain further traction in 2027.

Borrowing for energy efficiency improvements or with a low carbon footprint could trim

Moreover, banks will repay €67bn in EUR sustainable debt in 2027 up from €54bn in

amortisation, look-back periods and changes in use-of-proceeds criteria may prevent a 
full refinancing with new sustainable debt. In 2026, banks replaced 70% of their 
sustainable redemptions partially or fully with new sustainable supply. A comparable 
trend for 2027 would already cover close to half of our supply estimate for next year.

Corporate Environmental, Social and Governance (ESG) issuance is set for another

We expect corporate ESG supply to reach €72bn in 2027 
We pencil in €72bn in sustainable corporate issuance, slightly higher than our 2026 full-
year estimate of €70bn. Net supply is also higher at €41bn, up from €34bn year-on-
year, with redemptions falling €5bn YoY to €31bn next year. Supply is likely to remain 
supported by existing issuers refinancing redemptions and expanding their ESG curves, 
with utilities expected to remain active, as well as by new issuers entering the market. 
Over the past two years, 80-90% of issuance has been green, and we think this trend will 
continue next year. 
Utilities accounted for 66% of all issuance so far this year. Given their substantial capital 
expenditure plans, this dynamic is unlikely to change in 2027. The dominance of utilities 
in ESG issuance also means that sustainable hybrids will remain commonplace. So far in 
2026, around 25% of sustainable issuance from utilities has come in the form of hybrids, 
as companies seek to protect their credit profiles and ratings against the impact of 
significant investment pipelines.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it
